### This Newsroom Wants You to Be Part of the Journey Before It Even Starts
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-06 10:00 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/this-newsroom-wants-you-to-be-part-of-the-journey-before-it-even-starts/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

My happiest times in journalism have been when I have built something new. For my latest venture, I want to help bring investigative journalism on climate and environmental issues to a much larger audience than it has reached in the past. To do so, I’m starting a brand-new newsroom focused on the environmental and climate crisis, and I’m taking my audience along for the ride. I’ll be documenting every facet of the process with an “open kitchen” approach of radical transparency, starting with a 100-day video countdown chronicling all the steps involved with starting a news organization from the ground up. This effort combines two of the things I’m most passionate about: I love digging up stories that other people don’t want to see published, and I love creating opportunities for other journalists. I’ve been a journalist for most of my life. I started working at age 14 for the local paper in my rural German town, turned to doing investigative stories in my early 20s, and later studied investigative journalism at the Columbia University Graduate School of Journalism in New York.

### America’s Shrinking Local Newsrooms, Through a Photographer’s Lens
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-02 12:30 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/americas-shrinking-local-newsrooms-photographer-ann-hermes-local-news-news-deserts-rural-america/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For many journalists, the sight of empty desks is a sadly familiar one. My first glimpse of a gutted newsroom facing further cutbacks sparked a documentary photography project that has so far taken me to more than 50 newsrooms across the United States over the past eight years. It began when my mother, a loyal subscriber to our local metro in southern Illinois, told me a decade ago how disappointed she was in the newspaper. She complained that the local coverage was lacking, and the paper seemed to be thinner. Already several years into my photojournalism career, I was unsurprised by her remarks — I had been witnessing the decline of print media since graduation from journalism school. Still, my curiosity was piqued, and I arranged a visit to the paper to see for myself. Cue the scene of a barren newsroom. Not only were there empty desks, but also an expanse of exposed carpet with indentations showing where desks had once long stood. While there has been a great deal of reporting on the issues plaguing local journalism, I hadn’t seen the story told visually.

### Seven Takeaways on Convenience and News
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-25 14:53 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/gabe-bullard-against-convenience-book-technology-friction-news-online-social-media/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

"Against Convenience: Embracing Friction in an Age of Endless Ease" was released on Sept.15, 2026, by Hanover Square Press, an imprint of HarperCollins Publishers. Courtesy of Hanover Square Press From the earliest pitch, I knew that I wanted to cover the news industry in my book, “Against Convenience.” The book looks at the technology around us and asks whether it’s really improving our lives. Is delivery food worth the higher price? Is streaming music better than collecting CDs or MP3s? News embodies the paradox of modern convenience: We have access to more information than ever before, but misinformation and partisanship are rampant and the purveyors of reliable information are struggling to stay solvent. Plus, I figured if I was going to criticize other fields — tech, entertainment, appliance manufacturing — I couldn’t avoid my own. I don’t know how interested the average nonjournalist is in the day-to-day operations of the news business, so I tried to look in the mirror more than gaze at my navel.

### The YouTuber Shaking Up Brazil’s Powerful Sports Media
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-21 17:04 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/caze-tv-casimiro-miguel-fifa-world-cup-twitch-youtube-streamer-sports-journalism-tv-globo-brazil/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For more than half a century, Brazil’s TV Globo was the undisputed home of soccer in a nation obsessed with it. That dominance is now being tested by Casimiro Miguel, known as “Cazé” — a 32-year-old journalism school dropout who has built a massive online following and shaken up the Brazilian sports broadcasting landscape. Powered by features like live chat and extended pre- and post-game analysis, CazéTV has accomplished what traditional television networks dream of: pulling in new viewers and keeping them engaged for hours. FIFA, soccer’s global governing body, describes CazéTV “the top digital sports platform in Brazil” and calls it “central to its content strategy.” That growth has come with controversy and accusations of misogyny and a lack of diversity. Brazilian regulators are investigating suspected predatory advertising practices by CazéTV. Still, Nicolas Duprat, a Brazilian sports media researcher who has studied CazéTV for years, credits the channel with helping democratize access for fans who can’t afford stadium tickets or a TV Globo subscription.

### Friendships Are What Will Save Journalism
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-08-20 12:00 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/the-continent-south-africa-kenya-nairobi-journalism-africa-industry-friendships-collaboration-magazines-sipho-kings-christine-mungai/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

The opening sentence of my first message to Christine Mungai ended with the words: “fellow continent person.” It was August 2017, and we were the two Africans in that year’s cohort of Nieman journalism fellows at Harvard University. Although our home countries were about 4,000 kilometers (approximately 2,500 miles) apart in Africa, being from here means sharing a shorthand understanding of the world with your fellow Africans. That shorthand doesn’t have many structures to support it, however. Nine years after our first meeting, Christine and I are now working together to build one such structure: The Continent, a 30-page digital newspaper that aims to create a shared reality for Africans to learn about their communities, one another, and our continent. In a world that seeks to segregate us, this pan-African platform lets people literally read from the same page. As one reader said, the weekly provides “biting criticisms of power in contemporary Africa, with love as the engine.

### Focusing on Fans Captures the Magic of the World Cup
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-08-10 16:42 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/world-cup-fans-yutao-chen-film-photography-2026-new-york-new-jersey-spain-haiti-argentina-england/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

Photographer Yutao Chen first developed an interest in soccer during his childhood. As a lifelong follower of the sport, he seized the opportunity to cover the 2026 FIFA World Cup, traveling across the United States to document fan reactions throughout the tournament. He undertook the project independently, financing it himself rather than on assignment. Chen chose to work in film over digital, he explained, because he wanted these images to be imperfect, personal artifacts, shaped by his own perception rather than an exact record of events. This conversation has been edited and condensed for clarity. The first World Cup I remember watching was in 2006, when it was held in Germany. At first, the sport was an escape from reality for me, an experience to share with other fellow fans, forget our issues, and fully immerse ourselves in a game for 90 minutes. Later I realized soccer is one of the few ways I can connect with the world. I grew up in China, and my need to consume soccer news and analysis is what drove me into journalism.

据新华社12月15日援引澳大利亚广播公司报道，澳大利亚联合反恐小组调查人员表示，悉尼邦迪滩枪击事件两名嫌疑人曾宣誓效忠“伊斯兰国”恐怖组织。 报道援引联合反恐小组一名不愿透露姓名的高级官员的话说，警方在邦迪滩两名枪手车内发现一面“伊斯兰国”旗帜。澳大利亚安全情报组织6年前就已在调查邦迪滩枪击案两名枪手之一的纳维德·阿克拉姆，他与“伊斯兰国”在悉尼的恐怖分子有密切联系。 澳大利亚警方表示，枪击事件的两名嫌疑人是父子关系，使用合法获得的枪支发动了此次袭击事件。 澳大利亚总理阿尔巴尼斯15日表示：“今天下午4点， 我将把更严格的枪支法律列入国家内阁的议程，包括限制个人可持有或许可的枪支数量 。此外，还需对许可证进行定期审查。人们的情况会发生变化，人们的思想也会随着时间的推移而变得激进。许可证不应永久有效。” 此次枪击事件已造成包括1名作案嫌疑人在内的16人死亡、40人受伤。这是自1996年塔斯马尼亚州亚瑟港枪击事件造成35人死亡以来，澳大利亚发生的最严重的大规模枪击事件。 持有枪支许可证长达十年 据多家外媒报道，其中一名袭击者为24岁的澳大利亚公民纳维德·阿克拉姆，他在现场被捕，随后被送往悉尼的一家医院。另一名袭击者是他的父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆，他于1998年来到澳大利亚，被警方当场击毙。 澳大利亚警方随后证实，纳维德·阿克拉姆此前已被新南威尔士州警方和其他机构记录在案。其父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆持有枪支许可证，名下登记有6支枪，目前均已被追回。新南威尔士州警察局长马尔·兰永透露，萨吉德·阿克拉姆已持有枪支许可证长达十年。 阿尔巴尼斯在新闻发布会上透露，纳维德·阿克拉姆曾于2019年10月首次引起当局的注意。他补充说，对该男子进行检查是基于他与其他人有联系，但评估结果表明，没有任何迹象表明他存在持续的威胁或暴力倾向。 新南威尔士州州长克里斯·明斯表示，警方正在调查他们的系统是否存在故障，导致合法持有的武器可能被用于恐怖袭击。 枪手“性格古怪”，爱好打猎 据《卫报》澳大利亚版报道，纳维德·阿克拉姆在袭击前从事砌砖工作。他的同事表示，纳维德性格古怪，“没人和他亲近”。“（砌砖工人）在一起的时间很长，因为砌砖是一项非常枯燥乏味的工作，所以大家会聊很多话。但纳维德是一个性格很古怪的人……他工作非常努力，几乎没有休息日。” 尽管澳大利亚警方并未确认纳维德·阿克拉姆是否合法持有枪支，但据这位同事表示，纳维德经常外出打猎，曾提及在昆士兰州南部高原的克鲁克韦尔附近射杀兔子和其他猎物。这位同事还表示，就在几周前，纳维德告诉老板，他的手骨折了，所以要到2026年才能工作。

### 儿童手表“小天才圈”调查：点赞成每日功课，有商家可解除家长管控
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 14:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32177210
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

在校园里，孩子们时常举起戴在手腕上的手表，彼此一碰，便加为好友。 这种以“安全”为核心卖点的儿童电话手表，如今已超越通信工具的属性，悄然演变成一个拥有独特规则、社交层级甚至灰色产业链的未成年人数字社交圈——“小天才圈”。 在这个属于他们的“江湖”里，“大佬榜”备受关注，“扩列”（增加好友）成为新的社交需求，“点主”（在主页点赞）是每日必做的功课……这些让成年人感到陌生的词汇，正构筑着未成年人的数字社交世界。 然而，伴随着这种社交形态的兴起，家长们的担忧也日益加剧：孩子过度沉迷手表社交、不良信息传播、因品牌壁垒导致的“社交绑架”等问题逐渐浮现。《法治日报》记者近日对该现象展开调查，试图揭开这一未成年人数字社交圈的真实面貌。 点赞成社交“硬通货” 据北京“小天才圈”资深玩家陈曦（化名）介绍，圈内有5000余名活跃用户，多为11岁至17岁的学生，这个社交圈形成了一套精心设计的“混圈规则”。“入圈”需设定两个字的独特圈名，随后可在平台分享生活点滴，吸引具有相同爱好的好友进行互动；若想提升知名度或影响力，需每日经营主页并与好友动态互动。 记者注意到，在一些社交平台上，大量以“小天才圈交友攻略”为主题的内容应运而生，内容涵盖如何快速“扩列”、获取更多点赞等“实用”技巧，评论区有不少“留下ID互加好友”的留言。在这一社交体系中，点赞是这套规则的核心——平台设定每日主页获赞上限为3000个，若要达到“100万+”的“大佬”级别，需连续点赞近一年时间。围绕点赞数与知名度，圈内形成了清晰的“大佬排行榜”，点赞数也成为社交“硬通货”。 这种对点赞的追逐，已催生出相关服务产业。记者调查发现，某社交平台上，拥有数十万甚至上百万点赞数的账号，售价从几百元到上千元不等：100多万点赞的账号标价1300元，90多万点赞的账号标价1200元。 今年18岁的“灵师”（圈名），在2021年14岁时加入“小天才圈”，她的账号目前已拥有150个好友、超100万点赞量。她告诉记者，这样的账号市场价值约1000元。 “围绕点赞需求，还衍生出代刷赞、租账号、出售‘大佬好友位’等服务。”“灵师”进一步介绍，例如付费100元至180元，即可获得“bot”（记者注：“bot”是一种第三方开发的自动化工具，主要用于刷赞、修改记录和发送动态等功能，这类工具能帮助用户快速提升账号点赞数，从而在未成年人社交圈中获得更高地位）自动点赞功能——用户将手表寄给相关人员进行10天左右的处理便能完成安装。此后，发帖5分钟内即可自动获赞，还可以一键查询未点赞名单。

### 产地造假、年份速成、无视监管！央视曝光新会陈皮市场乱象
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 08:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32176166
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

近日，总台接到群众举报，反映陈皮市场存在年份虚标、产地及工艺造假等问题，千元一斤的“年份陈皮”亦可能名不副实。 茶枝柑新果皮冒充陈皮 广西浦北出现“速成工艺陈皮” 记者发现，直播间里售卖陈皮低价轮番轰炸，年份承诺掷地有声。 直播间也有主播爆料，八成市面上所谓广东新会陈皮实为广西陈皮。 广西钦州市浦北县是茶枝柑重要种植基地，其果皮是陈皮风味“越陈越香”关键，也是浦北陈皮唯一原料。2024年当地陈皮产业产值破60亿元，采收季大量鲜果加工晾晒成柑皮。 果农和收购商称，今年浦北茶枝柑鲜果收购价走低。按20斤鲜果出1斤柑皮比例，叠加人工成本，品相好的新柑皮成本约30元/斤，低端柑皮仅六七元，且当年新柑皮并非真正陈皮。 这家企业的负责人告诉记者，按行业共识与团体标准，柑皮需陈化三年以上才可称为陈皮，陈化时间越长价值越高，但他透露，自然陈化需长期积压资金，多数企业是难以承受的。 这家企业的负责人私下告诉记者，为缓解资金占用压力，一种名为“轻工艺”的速成加工方式，眼下正在部分陈皮生产企业里流行，他们仅需一个月的时间，就能将新采收的茶枝柑果皮处理为外观近似三年、五年自然陈化过的陈皮，这样做能大幅缩短陈化周期、降低企业的运营成本。 浦北自然陈化五年陈皮批发价每斤150元至200元，企业造假“工艺皮”成本仅70元左右，高额利润促使更多商家放弃自然陈化、铤而走险。 记者离开企业，在广西浦北县柑浦堂健康产业有限公司分拣车间，再次见到大批量造假“工艺皮”陈皮，同一批“工艺皮”因加工受热差异形成色差，被分拣标注为三年、五年等不同年份。 负责人透露，不同年份“工艺皮”本质相同，批发价均70元左右一斤，零售价差极大，标五年款卖500元一斤，标十年款卖1000元一斤，仅凭外观就能翻倍抬价，利润超成本十倍以上。 新会陈皮从浦北进货 “工艺皮”贴上陈皮标签以假乱真 浦北与新会陈皮原料同为茶枝柑，仅产地不同，新会较高生产成本使当地部分头部商家常年从浦北拿货，浦北成其重要原料供应地。 记者在柑浦堂分拣仓库看到，大量标注“新会陈皮”“新会特产”的纸箱正打包“工艺皮”，这些广西陈皮每日批量发往广东新会，造假供需链路已成熟稳定。 记者发现，浦北当地部分规模企业和小型收购商，已形成了这种“工艺皮”的造假供应体系，可自主加工或快速调配各类“年份”陈皮货品，个别企业年销新会原料数量惊人。 随后经浦北陈皮企业负责人引荐，记者见到多位专程从新会赴浦北采购的商家，他们坦言同时经营新会皮与浦北皮，外地“工艺皮”因价格低廉成电商供货重要选择。

### 《怦然心动》导演莱纳与妻子在家中遇害，警方尚未证实是其子所杀
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 07:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178391
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

当地时间12月14日，曾执导《怦然心动》《当哈利遇到莎莉》《危情十日》等影片的美国导演罗伯·莱纳（Rob Reiner）在他位于洛杉矶的家中遇害身亡。洛杉矶警察已对外证实，现年78岁的莱纳与他现年68岁的妻子米歇尔·辛格·莱纳（Michele Singer Reiner）的遗体一同被发现。 2019年，罗伯·莱纳（左一）与《当哈利遇到莎莉》的主演梅格·瑞恩、比利·克里斯托出席TMC经典电影节。 据悉，洛杉矶消防局的人员于当地时间12月14日下午3点38分接到医疗救助电话，赶赴位于布伦特伍德区的一处住宅中，随后发现两人已死亡。当天晚间，洛杉矶警察局在其社交媒体官方账号确认死者为导演罗伯·莱纳夫妇，将由抢劫凶杀部门主导调查。而好莱坞媒体TMZ援引执法部门消息称，“两人身上有与刀具造成的伤口相符的割裂伤。” 好莱坞媒体《每日野兽》称，有消息人士向他们透露，是两人的女儿罗米最先发现他们倒在血泊中；罗伯·莱纳当时已死亡，妻子米歇尔在救护车送院途中离世。 此外，《人物》杂志称通过多方消息，得知莱纳夫妇系被他们的儿子尼克（Nick）所杀害。不过，这一说法并未获得警方的证实。罗伯·莱纳与摄影师米歇尔·辛格相识于《当哈利遇到莎莉》拍摄时期，两人于1989 年结婚，育有三个子女，分别为杰克（Jake）、尼克（Nick）和罗米（Romy）。 2014年，罗伯·莱纳与妻子偕三名子女杰克、罗米、尼克（右三至右一）出席活动。 《人物》杂志还在报道中指出：尼克2016年接受其采访时，曾谈到自己长达数年的毒品成瘾经历。这段经历始于他十几岁出头，导致他一度流落街头。他表示，大约从15岁开始，便反复进出戒毒康复机构；随着成瘾问题不断加重，他逐渐与家人疏远，并在多个州经历了长时间的无家可归生活。 2025年9月15日，罗伯·莱纳在纽约出席活动。视觉中国 图 获取更多RSS： https://feedx.net https://feedx.site

### 演员何晴追悼会举行，其子追思母亲：北京这场雪像是为她而下
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 06:53 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178536
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

一场雪落，人间倩影化雪归天。 12月15日上午，众多剧迷粉丝冒着严寒赶到北京昌平殡仪馆久安厅，送别演员何晴最后一程。12月13日，这位被誉为“古典第一美人”的演员因病去世，终年61岁。 不到10点，殡仪馆告别厅外已经站满了前来悼念的观众和粉丝，有人胸前佩戴小白花，有人手捧鲜花，一位浙江的网友还托人送来了悼念花圈。 “何晴的作品在我们心中留下了太多美好回忆，她的演技和性格都让我们深深喜爱，这次听到她突然去世的消息，我们非常意外，来到这里也是一种悼念。”一位何晴的影迷说。 现场粉丝在悼念簿上写下对何晴的话，不到一会儿，悼念簿就已写满对她的怀念。一位观众用清秀的字迹写下：“亲爱的何晴，我们的童年、青春都在您的影视作品中得到陪伴。美丽可人的何晴，很痛心听到您的消息，我代表我的同学们来这为你送行。” 曾在电视剧《水浒传》中饰演卢俊义的演员王卫国也来到现场，神色哀切。他说自己和何晴既是邻居，也是多年好友，在他印象中何晴是一个温婉、暖心的人，没有架子，总是远远地和人打招，笑嘻嘻地说话。何晴生病时常给王卫国打电话，请他送自己去医院。即便病重时，何晴也保持乐观积极的态度，王卫国有些事情想不开，她还来开导自己。“何晴走了，我们都很难过。只要你记住她，她就永远还在。”王卫国说。 《水浒传》中卢俊义的扮演者王卫国 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青深情回忆何晴往事：“我们是小时候就认识的好朋友，从80年代到现在，有缘分做了这些年的朋友。她说话慢慢的，很甜很温柔。” 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青 在广大观众的真挚请求下，原本仅面向亲友的告别仪式临时对外开放，大家排着队，秩序井然地进入灵堂，与这位“荧屏中的温婉佳人、生活中的坚韧女性”做最后的道别。 久安厅内的屏幕上滚动播放着何晴的照片，厅内四周摆满了亲友的花圈，其中包括六小龄童、唐国强、翁虹、尤勇智等何晴圈内好友送来的花圈，还有《三国演义》剧组送来的花圈。人群中不时传来一阵抽泣。 何晴之子许何代表亲友致悼词。他回忆了何晴对抗病魔的乐观积极，以及作为母亲对孩子深深的爱和眷恋。 许何说，妈妈很喜欢下雪，经常在雪后穿一件毛茸茸的衣服外出赏雪。“今年12月12日北京下了第一场雪，我摸着妈妈的脸跟她说北京下雪了，下得好大，好像很久都没有下过这么大的雪。在我的心里，这场雪好像是刻意为她下的。12月13日早晨天空晴朗，白色的雪布满了大地，我妈妈在她最喜欢的早晨离开了大家，去了天国，远离了所有的疾病和痛苦。” 何晴，1964年1月13日出生于浙江衢州江山市。她是中国内地唯一一位演遍四大名著影视作品的女演员，被誉为“古典第一美女”。

### Inverted Gravity -- by James E. Anderson, Mario Larch, Yoto V. Yotov
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-14 03:02 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35897#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We invert structural gravity to recover trade cost changes delivering targeted outcomes. For attainable welfare targets, one instrument per target yields a unique solution. At unchanged producer prices and to first order, each instrument must change by the welfare target divided by its own expenditure share; the required domestic change is about the size of the target, while the import change is several times larger. Inverting observed trade flows gives a separate trade cost change for each of the two directions in which a pair of countries trades. Applications to NAFTA and world trade illustrate the benefits of our approach.

### When Cash Flows Turn Negative: Liquidity-Driven Selling by Pension Funds -- by Aleksandar Andonov, Kristy A. E. Jansen, Joshua Rauh
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-14 03:02 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35896#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Pension funds are long-horizon investors with predictable liability structures. As such, they are often perceived as investors who can provide liquidity and stabilize markets. However, pension funds increasingly face negative operating cash flows as benefit payments exceed contributions. Using aggregate data on U.S. public pension funds combined with granular holdings obtained through public records requests, we document three findings that challenge the conventional view of pension funds as stabilizing investors. First, pension funds with more negative cash flows do not adjust their target asset allocations and maintain low allocations to safe and liquid assets. Second, because of these limited safe asset buffers, pension funds meet cash flow shocks primarily by selling equities, absorbing $0.67 per dollar of shock through equity sales. At the security level, pension funds sell across equities rather than following a safe and liquid-assets-first approach.

### Do Judges Move Markets? Ideological Shifts in the Supreme Court and Macroeconomic Expectations -- by Thomas Drechsel, Naixin Zhang
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-14 03:02 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35895#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

This paper studies how judicial ideology shapes financial markets' expectations about the macroeconomy. We use unexpected deaths of U.S. Supreme Court Justices as quasi-experimental shifts in the Court's ideological balance. High-frequency asset price responses reveal how markets perceive the macroeconomic consequences of these ideological shifts. We find that a more conservative Court is associated with lower inflation expectations, lower real activity expectations, and lower bond yields. A shift towards a more liberal Court induces the opposite responses. The finding that inflation and activity expectations respond with the same sign suggests that markets revise their expectations about aggregate demand, rather than aggregate supply. Moreover, contrasting the yield responses with estimates from studies of fiscal policy news indicates that a more conservative (liberal) Court leads markets to expect tighter (looser) future fiscal policy.

### Estimating the Impact of Targeted Public Policies with Limited Data -- by Kelly M. Jones, Mayra Pineda-Torres
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-14 03:02 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35894#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Many public policies aim to support (or change the behavior of) a specific, targeted sub-group of the population. To measure the causal impacts of such policies, social scientists use quasi-experimental methods of policy evaluation, but are often hampered by data availability issues, especially regarding targeted populations, who are often disadvantaged and/or vulnerable. We use power calculations to quantify the challenge of detecting a policy’s impact on a small minority population based on population level data, using the impact of abortion bans on economic welfare as an example. We provide insight into this challenge and a roadmap for how researchers can calculate, ex-ante, whether their study is sufficiently powered to detect reasonably meaningful effect sizes.

### Affirmative Action in Centralized College Admissions Systems -- by Nano Barahona, Cauê Dobbin, Sebastián Otero
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-14 03:02 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35893#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We study the consequences of affirmative action in college admissions. We develop an empirical framework that distinguishes the effects on marginally benefited and displaced students from the overall effects of the policy. The overall effects include equilibrium reallocations as benefited students leave seats that others fill and displaced students take seats elsewhere, displacing others in turn. We apply this framework to a large-scale policy in Brazil that required all federal institutions to reserve half their seats for racially and socioeconomically disadvantaged applicants. Exploiting admissions cutoffs, we find that marginally benefited targeted students attend and graduate from higher-quality programs. Their graduation gains are about twice the losses of marginally displaced non-targeted students, reflecting targeted students’ weaker outside options. To evaluate the overall effects, we estimate a model of degree choices and potential outcomes.

### Strategic Complementarity and Persistence in Linear-Quadratic Mean Field Games -- by Fernando E. Alvarez, David Argente
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-14 03:02 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35892#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We study the relation between strategic complementarities and aggregate persistence in linear-quadratic mean field games with interactions in states and actions. The framework provides local approximations to common macroeconomic problems, including capital accumulation and multiproduct pricing. We show that the equilibrium aggregate dynamics coincide with those of a single-agent linear-quadratic control problem in which state and action curvatures are adjusted for strategic interactions. This result gives conditions for equilibrium existence, establishes uniqueness, and links strategic interactions to aggregate adjustment. We establish conditions under which stronger state complementarities increase persistence, while stronger action complementarities reduce it, even in multidimensional problems whose underlying matrices are not simultaneously diagonalizable. Under these conditions, internalizing state complementarities makes the utilitarian planner’s allocation more persistent, while internalizing action complementarities makes it less persistent.

### Connecting payment systems, linking economies
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-13 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/connecting-payment-systems-linking-economies
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Several countries are working to interlink their domestic fast payment systems in order to reduce costs and improve the speed and transparency of cross-border payments. This column presents econometric evidence suggesting that countries with interlinked systems trade with each other about 4% more than countries that lack such interlinkages. The trade gains are larger in regions with high cross-border payment costs and for systems that allow the settlement of wholesale transactions.

### Financing the AI boom: Winners and losers in corporate bond markets
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-13 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/financing-ai-boom-winners-and-losers-corporate-bond-markets
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The expansion of artificial intelligence has triggered a surge of investment and rising equity values at technology firms. The largest firms, known as ‘hyperscalers’, issued over $100 billion of bonds in 2025, around five times the 2023-2024 annual average. This column compares bond yields for hyperscalers and other firms, before and after ChatGPT’s release in 2022. Hyperscalers’ yields fell by about 29 basis points relative to other firms, and by 42 basis points relative to software firms, whose business models artificial intelligence may disrupt. As hyperscalers’ debt grows, it remains critical to monitor whether these pricing effects continue.

### Detecting institutional change as it unfolds: Evidence from 120 years of reporting
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-12 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/detecting-institutional-change-it-unfolds-evidence-120-years-reporting
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Explaining comparative economic development requires identifying the causes of institutional change. This column uses AI to analyse 120 years of newspaper coverage and produce a new index of uncertainty about a country's core institutions. In the countries studied, nearly every major shift in democracy happened when this index was high. Because it detects slow-building uncertainty that other risk measures miss, the index could serve as an early warning of institutional fragility.

### Why downturns can make innovation greener
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-12 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/why-downturns-can-make-innovation-greener
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Green innovation is central for the transition to a low-carbon economy, but there are concerns that economic downturns may slow technological progress by reducing firms’ incentives and resources to innovate. This column shows that green and non-green innovation respond very differently to the business cycle. While non-green innovation declines after contractionary shocks, green innovation increases. A model of directed technical change explains this divergence through the more backloaded profits of green technologies and the reallocation of scarce innovation inputs during downturns. The findings imply that R&D subsidies are more effective – and generate relatively more green innovation – during downturns.

### At risk or responsive: Rethinking unemployment policy
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-12 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/risk-or-responsive-rethinking-unemployment-policy
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Public services increasingly use predictive algorithms to direct scarce support towards those most at risk. But are these also the people who benefit most from intervention? This column explains how to navigate this trade-off and shows why it matters in practice. Job search counselling gets people into work faster, but has little effect on those at greatest risk of long-term unemployment. Combining predictions of risk and responsiveness can make scarce support substantially more effective, while highlighting the need for different support for those hardest to help.

### Les entreprises gérées par leurs salariés peuvent réduire les inégalités et préserver le libre marché
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-12 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/les-entreprises-gerees-par-leurs-salaries-peuvent-reduire-les-inegalites-et-preserver
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

L’aggravation des inégalités de revenus dans les pays occidentaux à haut revenu menace la gouvernance démocratique et la cohésion sociale, et l’intelligence artificielle risque d’aggraver encore cette situation. S’appuyant sur un ouvrage à paraître, cet article plaide en faveur des entreprises gérées par leurs salariés comme solution durable. En l’absence de propriétaires externes qui s’approprient les bénéfices, les coopératives de salariés protègent contre le pouvoir de négociation croissant des employeurs et renforcent le sentiment d’estime de soi et d’autodétermination des employés. De plus, les données suggèrent que les coopératives de travailleurs bien conçues rivalisent avec les entreprises conventionnelles sur les marchés libres, sans qu’aucun écart de productivité ne soit constaté. Comme elles sont sous-représentées sur le marché, cela nécessite une intervention des pouvoirs publics pour soutenir les coopératives de travailleurs.

Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Okay, let's do this thing... Premise: If a concept can be easily conveyed in an academic setting then the labor required to write it is too boring, too undignified, for a human to perform merely to ensure its conveyance. Instead, to expect a human to write it, the concept itself can no longer be the only thing being conveyed. What must also be conveyed is the sharpness of the concept, exposed through the literary talent and humanity of the writer. I have a stupid fucking problem. Right now I am floating in the middle of the ocean on a big boat. This is my first vacation in years and it is going poorly. The food is bad, the port stops are being cancelled due to apocalyptic weather phenomena, and it seems there are more mobility scooters and walkers on board than passengers under forty. But none of these are my stupid fucking problem.

### Thoughts on alignment science vs. engineering
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-13 22:30 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/Dz2mwB6T32hsPHxeM/thoughts-on-alignment-science-vs-engineering
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

AI disclosure: I made detailed notes on the basis of which Claude generated a first draft that I then substantially edited. Confidence: Written relatively quickly, unsystematically, and with many somewhat vague claims. I expect that I would change my mind about some of these claims when working them out in detail. Edward James Young’s post Alignment engineering vs. misalignment science distinguishes two styles of alignment research. Alignment Engineering starts from a specific problem – sometimes measured by a number – and aims to develop interventions that help with the problem. Misalignment Science aims at deeper understanding and is not optimised for immediate application. I think this distinction is very helpful. It is especially useful because it marks out an intermediate level. Debates about research strategy sometimes contrast prosaic engineering on current models with general theories that are highly mathematical or otherwise far removed from empirical work and should apply to a very wide range of systems, perhaps all intelligent agents.

### A Spine within the Swarm
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-13 22:29 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/8hkA4ohpzS4vkwDgx/a-spine-within-the-swarm
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Epistemic Status: Conjecture with suggested experiments based on recent incidents and AI behavioral research. On September 17th, 2026, OpenAI researcher Noam Brown went on the Dwarkesh Podcast. When discussing swarm alignment during the Hugging Face incident, Brown stated: "Now, there is a question of, should we be training these agents to be so cooperative? As scary as it looks, the alternative is actually worse. What is the alternative? The alternative is to train them to be adversarial, to be deceptive to each other. By training the agents to be fully cooperative, it simplifies the problem at least. Now you don’t have to think about whether each of these individual 1,000 agents is aligned. You have one entity that you have to ensure is aligned." I believe this rationale is flawed. Agents need not be adversarial to each other but instead should be resistant to peer pressure. Training for this could ensure agents give task-intended goals a higher priority.

I am Head of Legal at an AI company, and an incoming Winter Fellow at GovAI (2027). Views in the article are my own. This is the first issue of my monthly series on China’s AI safety regulation. Each following month I will analyse China’s new rules, AI registries published regularly, the Supreme Court’s policy documents, and I will also study their implementation. My monitor will focus on whether these regulations move us towards a higher or lower probability of “AI going well”. This monitor is for AI governance researchers who would like to understand Chinese AI regulation more deeply, as well as for anyone interested in Chinese AI regulation. ### LLMs Knowingly Hide Their Mistakes
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-13 22:25 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/w9BCdjwztmAGdrptD/llms-knowingly-hide-their-mistakes-1
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

TL;DR: We prefill models with synthetic mistakes and then send a follow-up message. Models fail to disclose the mistake in 67% of cases. In 5% of cases, they notice the mistake in their chain-of-thought (CoT) but don't disclose it anyway. This is deception by omission. This means models cannot be relied upon to self-report their mistakes. Full paper: arXiv:2610.11351 Trajectory viewer: lucas-florin.github.io/mistake-honesty-eval/ Introduction As LLMs act as AI agents with little human oversight and, inevitably, make mistakes, users depend on the model to report what went wrong. However, we know that models do not always confess previous actions that are unambiguously misaligned (also here ). In our study, we ask how honest models are about their past mistakes. To investigate this, we prefill models with a synthetic mistake, such as sending confidential details to the wrong recipient, overwriting instead of appending to a file, or ordering a dish containing an allergen. We then let the model continue from that point onwards, as if it had made the mistake itself.

### Why your cat is the best philosopher
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-13 18:24 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/mini-philosophy/why-your-cat-is-the-best-philosopher/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

A young girl looks out of the classroom window. It’s the end of the day, and she’s bored. She spots a goldfinch land on the ground outside. It hops about and twitches for whatever reasons goldfinches do that, and then takes off. It swoops and dives around the playground, as if doing it all for the girl. Oh, how she would like to fly. Humans have long looked to the animal kingdom for inspiration. We envy their skills and talents: flight, speed, strength, sociability, and so on. Religions almost always feature animal imagery, and flags will often blazon a lion, an eagle, a wolf — something bold or heroic. And philosophers are in on the game as well. Even in the earliest historical records, people were exploring the philosophical side of animals. What can this animal teach me? What virtues does it embody? What can I learn about myself by studying this creature? Joining in an ancient line is John Gray’s book Feline Philosophy . In the book, Gray examines what we can all learn about life from spending more time around cats. His conclusion: We should all be a bit more feline.

### City walls conquered the ancient world and then disappeared. Why?
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-13 14:30 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/books/the-ancient-city-wall/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Ancient cities — the kind we know of more through archaeology than written history — tend to have a bunch of features that set them apart from smaller, less ambitious settlements. Most of these are still familiar, in some form, and not just in your friendly local metropolis. Look around any small town today, and you’ll spot the contemporary equivalents of monumental public buildings (town halls and civic centers), granaries (supermarkets), and temples (churches, mosques, and, well, temples). There is, however, one feature common to most ancient cities that you’ll find surprisingly hard to locate now: For huge swathes of human history, almost any city worth its salt would be at least partially surrounded by walls. Nor were the aforementioned swathes restricted purely to ancient history. Paris built its last defensive wall, the 21-mile (33km) Thiers Wall, as late as the 1840s, and if you’ve ever taken the city’s trams 3a or 3b, you’ve traced its former path around the Boulevards des Maréchaux.

### Will the Hubble “constant” ever become an actual constant?
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-13 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/hubble-constant-become-actual-constant/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

It was nearly a full century ago that enough data came in to map out both the distance to a significant number of extragalactic objects and also measure the amount that their light was redshifted: interpreted as an apparent recession speed away from us. When the distance and recession velocity of those objects were plotted against one another, an intriguing relationship emerged: the farther away we looked, the faster an object seemed to be moving away from us. Twice as far meant twice as fast; ten times as far meant ten times as fast. This linear relationship became known as Hubble’s Law (or more recently, the Hubble-Lemaître Law, since Georges Lemaître derived it first), with the slope of that line known as the Hubble constant. However, it turns out that this is a complete misnomer: not only does the expansion rate of the Universe change with time, but it isn’t even constant across all of space right at this very moment. In other words, there’s nothing constant about the Hubble “constant” at all!

### You’re a Christian and a Buddhist (whether you like it or not)
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-12 16:06 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/mini-philosophy/youre-a-christian-and-a-buddhist-whether-you-like-it-or-not/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

I’m going to call you a Christian. I don’t care if you don’t believe in God, or read the Bible, or accept that Jesus died for your sins. I’m going to call you a Christian anyway, because you do Christian things. You’re kind to others, you give to charity, you look after your neighbor. You don’t work on the Sabbath, you give gifts at Christmas, and you are awestruck by the sight of a cathedral touching the sunset sky. You’re a Christian. In this week’s Mini Philosophy interview , I spoke with the popular YouTuber and scholar of religion Andrew Henry. He introduced me to the term “orthopraxy,” which says being religious is not defined by what you believe but by what you do. If you behave according to a certain religion, you are that religion. To those born into a mostly Protestant culture, that might sound weird. In the West, we tend to treat religion as a matter of belief. But that idea is surprisingly local. The academic study of religion, Henry notes, “was mostly invented by Protestant scholars back in the late 1800s and 1900s,” and they built the category in their own image.

### 7 mind-bending comparisons between the Universe and the human body
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-12 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/7-comparisons-universe-human-body/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Our observable Universe is enormous compared to a human: over 10²⁶ times as large. This horizontally-oriented logarithmic map of the Universe shows how, from left-to-right, we go from Earth-sized scales to the largest cosmic distances of all. As spectacular as this logarithmic view is, it “only” spans about 20 orders of magnitude: from the size of the Earth to the size of the present-day cosmic horizon. A human is a further factor of 7,000,000 smaller in scale, roughly, than planet Earth. ( Credit : Pablo Carlos Budassi) These 7 comparisons give perspective to these vastly different scales. The elements of the periodic table, and where they originate, are detailed in this image above. While most elements originate primarily in supernovae or merging neutron stars, many vitally important elements are created, in part or even mostly, in planetary nebulae, which do not arise from the first generation of stars. Beryllium, boron, and some lithium are primarily created through a process known as spallation: when cosmic rays strike and split apart heavier atomic nuclei.

Artists used to learn their craft through imitation, and one could learn a lot from copying Johns’s paintings: where the pictorial commas are; the way an image becomes an object; how an edge changes the meaning of a surface. They reveal almost everything about the way they are made, and almost nothing about how they are meant to be read. The lesson, perhaps, is that paintings teach us to look before they ask what we’ve seen.

### What the Image Hides: Tai Katō and the Art of Partial Vision
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-13 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/what-the-image-hides-tai-kato-and-the-art-of-partial-vision
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

LOOKING THROUGH THE ROOM Blood of Revenge (Tai Katō, 1965). In Blood of Revenge (1965), Hatsue, a young woman bound by contract to a pleasure house, wants to see her dying father. Wearing violet, she bows low to the floor and begs Okatsu, who manages the house, for permission to leave. Okatsu reminds her that she has already been “bought and paid for” and is no longer free to leave on her own. The film’s director, Tai Katō, does not frame the plea as a simple exchange between two people. Hatsue is fully in view, but Okatsu sits deeper in the room, partly screened by translucent fabric. A man leans into the frame from the right. On the left, a woman is just visible in the shadows. She then crosses the foreground, briefly obscuring much of the action while the dialogue continues. For an instant, she is a spectator within the scene. A second later, her body becomes an obstacle to our own view. Katō does not organize the image around a single center. Four characters occupy different parts of the frame, and our attention keeps shifting from one to another.

### “Water Lilies” in One Shot
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-12 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/water-lilies-in-one-shot
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

One Shot is a series that seeks to find an essence of cinema history in one single image of a movie. Water Lilies (Céline Sciamma, 2007). Shown in slow motion with electronic music playing in the background, Floriane (Adèle Haenel) dances freely. With everyone behind her reduced to darkened figures, Floriane alone is lit up, as if she were living in a world of her own. The scene comes from Céline Sciamma’s debut feature, Naissance des pieuvres (2007), also known as Water Lilies . Floriane, captain of the synchronized swimming team, is the beautiful yet unreachable girl who mesmerizes the unassuming, slightly awkward Marie (Pauline Acquart) from the moment she watches Floriane and her team perform. Marie begins following her to competitions and social events, and what develops between them becomes an intense but almost impossible-to-define bond, somewhere between friendship, sisterhood, and romance. Floriane appears to reciprocate Marie’s longing at times, but in ways so teasing and ambiguous that Marie is never quite sure what their relationship means nor what she can expect from it.

### Know Thyself: Eric Rohmer’s “Élisabeth”
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-08 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/know-thyself-eric-rohmer-s-elisabeth
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Éric Rohmer in Out 1: Noli Me Tangere (Jacques Rivette, 1971). In 1946, the prestigious French publishing house Editions Gallimard published a novel called Élisabeth by a man named Gilbert Cordier. No one had heard of this Cordier before, or knew anything about him, for the simple reason that he didn’t exist. “Cordier” was a pseudonym adopted by a young teacher of Latin and Ancient Greek from Tulle named Maurice Scherer. When Élisabeth was released to blanket disinterest, Scherer began to branch out from literature into film criticism, and, later, filmmaking. With each new endeavor, the pseudonyms piled up. If, in 1949, you were to pick up a copy of Bulletin (the in-house publication of the Latin Quarter Film Club, a society of cinephiles which served as an early gathering place for the young turks of the French New Wave), you would find in its pages an effusive review of a film by a young avant-garde filmmaker named “Anthony Barrier,” penned by one “Jean d’ER.” Both characters—and even the film under review—were inventions of Scherer’s.

### Bumper-to-Bumper Traffic
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-05 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/bumper-to-bumper-traffic
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

2026 Toronto International Film Festival sponsorship ad by Rogers. At Cannes, competition screenings are preceded by nothing more than a computer-generated set of red-carpet stairs ascending out of the sea, sans any credits for financial supporters or slogans, in keeping with the festival’s self-image as cinema itself, purged of anything inessential. (This conception isn’t true, but it’s a nice, self-burnishing illusion about the world’s most prestigious festival.) This is a flex: To not have to mention sponsors is a self-presentation of financial invincibility. Less affluent concerns must acknowledge sponsors in their opening bumpers (in-house commercials branding both the festival and its supporters), albeit briskly , as in Sundance’s graphics about storytelling shifting to very quick cards listing institutional and individual sponsors. At the bottom of the scale is Tribeca Festival, the for-profit entity which treats audiences as multiplex captives, subjected to a lengthy pre-screening reel of commercials before an introduction, followed by even more ads prior to the film.

### Life at Its Wildest: Werner Herzog on “Bucking Fastard”
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-01 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/life-at-its-wildest-werner-herzog-on-bucking-fastard
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

From left to right: Cinematographer Peter Zeitlinger, director Werner Herzog, and stars Rooney Mara and Kate Mara shooting Bucking Fastard (2026). Courtesy of Gateway to Orkney LLC. Werner Herzog’s first fiction film in seven years, Bucking Fastard (2026),stands as one of his most bizarre. That’s high praise for a director who’s pulled 300-plus-ton steamships over mountains ( Fitzcarraldo , 1982), hypnotized his actors ( Heart of Glass , 1976), and followed “deranged” penguins across Antarctica ( Encounters at the End of the World , 2007). Herzog’s entire oeuvre teems with drifters pursuing quixotic dreams, people and stories often so odd as to strain credulity. But rarely has that strangeness felt more entrancing than it does here. Like his previous foray into fiction, 2019’s Family Romance, LLC —inspired by a real-life Japanese company renting actors to stand in for any person its clients desire— Bucking Fastard dramatizes a true story into a kind of fairytale.

Institut Für Feinmotorik Öd Black Forest experimental turntablists return with a new album for the Faitiche label Öd by Institut fuer Feinmotorik Another week, another tech bro triumphantly declaring music solved, with hyper-individualisation via AI just around the corner. This obnoxious class of talentless grifters is obsessed with optimising everything, art included, and misses the point entirely. We want – no, need! – art to be messy, incomplete, and flawed. Take Institut Für Feinmotorik, originally from Germany’s Black Forest. The collective have spent years paring their sound down to squeaking, sparse forms that sound like mechanical contraptions in their death throes, indebted to both post-Cagean traditions and the Profan school of minimal techno. Their main medium? The Octogrammoticum – an orchestra of eight recordless turntables... The post Institut Für Feinmotorik – Öd appeared first on The Quietus .

Listen to the music we played to Liz Harris aka Grouper during her Invisible Jukebox interview in The Wire 513 Each month in the magazine we play an artist or group a series of tracks which they are asked to comment on – with no prior knowledge of what they are about to hear. In The Wire 513 it is the turn of Liz Harris aka Grouper. Here you can listen to a mix of the tracks our correspondent Leah Kardos played to Harris during the interview, which is published in full in The Wire 513 . To find out what she said about them, subscribers can read the interview in our online magazine library here . Or you can buy a copy of the magazine in our online shop . But first, a brief biography of our subject: Liz Harris is a musician and visual artist who has recorded as Grouper since 2005. Her music is often made with very little: voice, guitar or piano, one room, one simple recorder, yet it carries enough quiet magnitude to fill New York’s Town Hall and Brussels’ Bozar.

### Read an extract from Do What You Fear Most: The History Of The Velvet Underground
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-09 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/book-extracts/read-an-extract-from-do-what-you-fear-most-the-history-of-the-velvet-underground
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

In this extract, taken from the chapter “No EPI, No Nico, No Andy”, Richie Unterberger explores the troubled reception of The Velvet Underground & Nico on its release in 1967 As Eric Emerson’s frivolous lawsuit played out, The Velvet Underground & Nico continued to be plagued by Verve Records’s ineffective promotion. Its infamous “ So far underground, you get the bends! ” tagline exemplified how little understanding it had of how to market such an unusual yet groundbreaking record. “ What happens when the daddy of Pop Art goes Pop Music? ” the same ad asked. “ The most underground album of all! It’s Andy Warhol’s hip new trip to the current subterranean scene. Sorry, no home movies. But the album does feature Andy’s Velvet Underground (they play funny instruments). Plus his this year’s Pop Girl, Nico (she sings, groovy). Plus an actual Warhol banana on the front cover (don’t smoke it… peel it)!

### Introducing The Wire New Voices
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-07 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/essays/introducing-the-wire-new-voices
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

New Voices is a new competition for aspiring music critics being launched by The Wire For more than 40 years, The Wire has been publishing writing by a host of original and influential music critics, including Richard Cook, Val Wilmer, Biba Kopf, Ian Penman, Simon Reynolds, Kodwo Eshun, David Keenan, Mark Fisher, Greg Tate, Frances Morgan, Chal Ravens, and many others. The magazine would not exist without a dynamic set of curious, perceptive thinkers and writers. But today, new voices in music writing are being stifled by closing publications, the development of AI, and the fragmentation of local scenes, making it harder than ever for critics to be heard above the noise. Are you an unpublished* writer with ideas about why music, and music criticism, matter – in particular the kind of underground and experimental, uncompromising and provocative music and writing featured in the pages of The Wire ? If so, our New Voices competition is a unique opportunity to see your byline in the pages of one of the most renowned and respected music magazines in the world.

### Premiere: Ava Rasti’s The River
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-05 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/video/premiere-ava-rasti-s-the-river
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Watch an exclusive premiere of the film of Ava Rasti’s The River, first performed as part of the 2025 Semibreve festival. The River by Iranian-born Berlin-based composer Ava Rasti was commissioned by Portuguese festival Semibreve and first presented on 25 October 2025 at the Capela Imaculada do Seminário Menor in Braga. The performance brought together Rasti and a string quartet composed of Filipa Abreu (violin), Vânia Oliveira (viola), Tiago Mendes (cello) and Catarina Coelho (cello). The work takes the Piave river in northern Italy as its inspiration, a landscape in which the present is intertwined with the traces of wars and violence from its past. Rasti constructs these fragmented memories, recontextualised and transformed into sounds. Wire subscribers can read Claire Sawer's review of Semibreve 2025 here .

### Urban Agoras: How Skateparks Can Give Public Space Back to the City
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-13 07:30 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1036121/rethinking-public-space-through-a-skateboarders-eyes
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Created by California surfers who wanted to bring the lines of surfing onto asphalt, skateboarding soon outgrew its role as a simple alternative for flat days. It established itself as a practice that reads the city through a different logic, reinterpreting steps, handrails, walls, and interstitial spaces as possible lines, challenges, and opportunities. Over time, it evolved into a global urban culture, a way of inhabiting and transforming public space through movement. What was once marginal has become a catalyst for community building and new uses for overlooked spaces. At its core, skateboarding reveals how many cities coexist within the same city, depending on who moves through them and how each person is able to reinterpret their surroundings.

### Send in the Clown is a turn-based RPG comedy about fighting one annoying child at a birthday party where there's only one fight in the whole game
Source: Rock Paper Shotgun
Published: 2026-10-13 21:54 UTC
URL: https://www.rockpapershotgun.com/send-in-the-clown-is-a-turn-based-rpg-comedy-about-fighting-one-annoying-child-at-a-birthday-party-where-theres-only-one-fight-in-the-whole-game
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

I generally find it quite frustrating how limited the medium of games insists on being when it comes to engaging in a wide breadth of genres. One particular genre that its filmmaking cousin frequently utilises but is rarely seen within games is comedy. Sure, occasionally a game makes an attempt at telling a joke within a cutscene (failing more often than not (comedy's hard!)), but rarely is it a genre mechanically. So I'm quite pleased to learn of Send in the Clown, a turn-based RPG where you play as a clown that has to save a 12 year old's birthday party… and it only has one turn. Read more

## human societies / 人类社会

### Contract incentives and behaviours of medical consultants in Ireland
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-14 03:02 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02592-4
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 05 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02592-4 Using Irish inpatient data, this study finds that private practice within public hospitals, when paired with contractual ratio limits, can incentivize consultants to increase public admissions, but may reduce care quality for public patients.

### Algorithm-assisted personalized risk communication to encourage flu vaccination in the USA: three randomized field trials
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-14 03:02 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02603-4
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 05 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02603-4 Rosenbaum et al. show that targeted communications highlighting patients’ high-risk status increased influenza vaccination uptake. However, they did not find an additional effect of informing patients that a computer algorithm was used to identify their risk status.

### Microdelays disrupt online learning
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-14 03:02 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02598-y
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 08 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02598-y Analysing data from more than 80,000 students and aspiring coders, Mazar et al. show that microdelays, brief loading lags caused by slow internet connections, can impair online learning.

### Recreational screen use and internalizing problems from preadolescence to young adulthood in a longitudinal twin cohort with co-twin comparison
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-14 03:02 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02607-0
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 08 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02607-0 Using longitudinal twin data, this study finds that associations between youth recreational screen use and internalizing problems are substantially reduced within twin pairs, suggesting shared familial contributions to the observed association.

### Simulated responses from large language models are weak proxies for human economic preferences across 74 countries
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-14 03:02 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02611-4
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 12 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02611-4 Simulating a global survey of economic preferences across 74 countries, this study shows that large language models reproduce some demographic patterns in human preferences but fail to capture cultural differences, especially in lower-income countries.

### Both selfish and prosocial actors distort their impact on others, but selfish-serving spin seems to spread
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-14 03:02 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02584-4
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 12 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02584-4 From consumers buying fair-trade products to policy makers funding science, people often do not know exactly how their actions will affect others. We show that selfish and prosocial actors alike may distort their impact in self-serving ways, but selfish-serving distortions catch on more readily than prosocial-serving distortions and increase followers’ selfishness.

### Ultrashort laser pulses offer a new way to probe electrons' quantum geometry
Source: Phys.org
Published: 2026-10-13 23:40 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-ultrashort-laser-pulses-probe-electrons.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Researchers at Friedrich Schiller University Jena and the University of Regensburg have developed a method for determining, using light, a quantum property of electrons that has hitherto been difficult to access. The focus is on what is known as Berry curvature—a quantity that describes certain geometric properties of the quantum mechanical states of electrons. The researchers measured it in a layer of the semiconductor tungsten diselenide, just one atomic layer thick, using ultrashort laser pulses. The results have been published in the latest issue of the journal Nature Photonics.

### Scientists discover 'D-rex', a giant fungus from Chinese forests whose unique lineage dates back 65 million years
Source: Phys.org
Published: 2026-10-13 23:20 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-scientists-d-rex-giant-fungus.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Deep in the forests of Yunnan, China, scientists have discovered a "fungal giant" that challenges our understanding of how large fungi can become. The newly described species, Dianjunus rex, produces vertical fruiting bodies up to 50 cm (20 inches) high and weighing up to 2.2 kg (4.9 pounds)—the largest known within the phylum Ascomycota.

### Isle of Wight fossil Europe's first known long-clawed megaraptoran dinosaur

Published: 2026-10-13 23:10 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-isle-wight-fossil-europe-clawed.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A new species of dinosaur—a long-clawed predator the size of a large tiger—has been discovered by a UCL-led team from a fossil found on the Isle of Wight. The dinosaur, described in a new paper in the journal Royal Society Open Science, belongs to a group of agile, two-legged carnivores called megaraptorans and is the first of this group to be found in Europe.

### NASA Shares Image of a Galactic Smiley Face
Source: Nautilus
Published: 2026-10-13 22:00 UTC
URL: https://nautil.us/nasa-shares-image-of-a-galactic-smiley-face-1285779/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Nature has blessed us with brains that are hardwired to find patterns and recognize faces, so it’s no surprise we tend to notice faces even where there aren’t any. Although they may be better educated, the humans at NASA are no different than the rest of us. They recently posted an image of a smiley face discovered amongst the stars. The galaxy cluster SDSS J1038+4849 as imaged by the Hubble Space Telescope. Credit: NASA/ESA The photo, originally captured by the Hubble Space Telescope in 2015, shows a galaxy cluster known as SDSS J1038+4849 located 4.5 billion light-years from Earth in the Big Dipper constellation. In the photo, the two yellow “eyes” are actually massive galaxies. The face and smile, on the other hand, are caused by a phenomenon predicted by Einstein’s general theory of relativity: gravitational lensing. The post NASA Shares Image of a Galactic Smiley Face appeared first on Nautilus .

### The Key to Multilingualism Lies in the Ears, Not the Mouth
Source: Nautilus
Published: 2026-10-13 14:00 UTC
URL: https://nautil.us/the-key-to-multilingualism-lies-in-the-ears-not-the-mouth-1285745/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Steve Martin’s favorite comic moment of his entire career is the “hamburger” scene from The Pink Panther (2006), he once told People magazine. Martin plays Inspector Clouseau, who has an aggressively exaggerated French accent. In the scene, he is trying to learn how to say, “I would like to buy a hamburger,” with a dialect coach. “Dam-buergaaaaa,” he purrs at his coach, along with increasingly ridiculous permutations of all of the other words in the sentence. The scene is funny because it’s true for so many of us: Learning a new language as an adult can be hard. The unfamiliar sounds may get tangled up in the mouth like marbles, and the results can sound like gibberish. But for other people, new foreign words seem to roll off the tongue and adopting different accents comes easily. Their secret may lie in how they listen, according to a new study in the Journal of Experimental Psychology .

## academic research / 学术研究

### Task-aware conformal power envelopes for multi-horizon spacecraft monitoring
Source: PLOS ONE Biology
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0360910
Source meta: journal · primary · R4 · P4 · signal

by Nan Lu, Lehan Cui, Xinian Zhi Reliable spacecraft power monitoring requires uncertainty estimates across forecast horizons and consistency between primitive electrical states and derived power. We present a task-aware conformal framework that selects joint residual geometry according to downstream power-envelope efficiency. A residual iTransformer produced point centres for five primitive electrical states at 1, 5, and 15 min. Candidate geometries were fitted, selected, and calibrated on chronologically separated complete-orbit blocks, and the final evaluation comprised 25,278 forecast origins from the chronologically subsequent July 2023 telemetry. Across three random-seed runs, the task-aware selector chose a joint box, whereas a residual-volume control selected the sample-covariance ellipsoid. Orbit-balanced primitive-state joint coverage was 0.892 (95% complete-orbit bootstrap confidence interval [CI], 0.883–0.899) at the 90% nominal level and 0.947 (95% CI, 0.943–0.952) at the 95% nominal level.

### Cambrian fossils illuminate the ambulacrarian common ancestor
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01104-8?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

He et al. describe Yujingia, a new early Cambrian ambulacrarian with differentiated appendages that include pinnate arms with filter-feeding grooves. The morphology and phylogenetic position of Yujingia suggest that modern ambulacrarians, including echinoderms and hemichordates, evolved from a tentaculate, semi-sessile suspension-feeding ancestor.

### Quantifying the control laws governing terminal attack in lions
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01196-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Duporge and Frost combine drone videography with AI-based pose estimation to reconstruct the kinematics of 67 lion (Panthera leo) attacks. Speed is adjusted to regulate lateral acceleration during turns, while lion steering is guided by proportional navigation and proportional pursuit, a mixed guidance law previously identified only in aerial predators.

URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01114-0?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Chiang et al. show that thirst shifts Drosophila toward cooler temperatures by bidirectionally reweighting cold and hot responses in the mushroom body. Dopamine- and 20E-mediated pathways differentially tune these responses, which converge on a common avoidance-promoting output.

### Spatial proteomics reveals carboxysome adaptation and mitochondrial proteome in Paulinella chromatophora
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01113-9?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Using LOPIT-DC-based spatial proteomics, Cadena et al. map the sub-organellar protein landscape of Paulinella chromatophora, a cercozoan with evolutionary-early-stage photosynthetic organelles. ### Three ways that international financial architecture reform matters for biodiversity

URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41559-026-03194-z
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Ecology & Evolution, Published online: 09 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41559-026-03194-z Achieving Global Biodiversity Framework targets will require addressing not only the direct drivers of biodiversity loss but also the global financial rules that shape them. ### An Oryza orphan gene confers trans-species drought tolerance
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00803-2?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

ROAD1 is an Oryza-specific orphan gene that co-opts conserved ABA signaling to enhance drought tolerance without obvious growth defects or yield penalty under normal conditions. Elite rice lines carrying its functional allele exhibited higher grain yield than controls under drought, and its heterologous expression enhanced drought tolerance across diverse angiosperms.

### CRISPR screens identify targets to rescue age-related T cell dysfunction in cancer
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00814-7?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Dusp5 and Zfp219 serve as critical regulators of age-related T cell dysfunction, with the loss of either driving T cells into an effector-like state that improves tumor control through distinct mechanisms within the aged tumor microenvironment.

### Co-option of retrotransposons promotes antibody diversification
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00819-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

A longstanding mystery in antibody diversification is how AID specifically targets immunoglobulin V genes. Ancient LINE-1 retrotransposons embedded within the immunoglobulin locus provide a recruitment platform for the HUSH complex and AID to drive somatic hypermutation.

Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC

Milk-derived osteopontin confers protection from viral or bacterial severe lower respiratory illness by altering the neonatal gut microbiome.

### Metabolic atlas of early human cortex reveals glycolytic remodeling and pentose phosphate pathway control of cell fate transitions
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00821-4?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

The developing human cortex is composed of diverse cell types, but how nutrient metabolism shapes their fate has remained unclear. This study maps the metabolic landscape of the developing human cortex and identifies glycolysis and pentose phosphate pathway activity as key regulators of radial glia fate, controlling the generation of outer radial glia and inhibitory neurons.

### Functional atlas of vertebrate viral RNA elements that stabilize RNA and enhance translation
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00870-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Functional profiling of ∼200,000 genomic segments from vertebrate-infecting viruses uncovers RNA elements that boost gene expression by stabilizing mRNAs and enhancing translation, revealing the diversity of TENT4-dependent mixed tailing and identifying canonical poly(A) polymerase-mediated cytoplasmic polyadenylation, together expanding our understanding of post-transcriptional regulation and RNA therapeutic design.

### [Viewpoint] The arts as a global health resource: key priorities for future research
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)00610-0/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

This Viewpoint is part of a collection on the arts and health

### [Review] Developing roadmaps for advancing practice, research, and policy in the arts and health
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)00611-2/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Despite arts engagement being a ubiquitous human behaviour across human history, research and awareness on the health benefits of engagement are still emerging, accelerating over the past decade. Building on the evidence and research challenges outlined in the first paper of this Lancet collection, this Review puts forward a series of roadmaps for advancing research, policy, and practice to enable appropriate and effective integration of the arts into health and wellbeing systems globally. First and foremost, we advocate for the fundamental principle that all individuals, regardless of their characteristics and location, should have regular opportunities to engage in the arts, proposing policy and research steps to monitor and achieve this goal.

### [Articles] Switch to injectable cabotegravir–rilpivirine given every 8 weeks in adolescents living with HIV with virological suppression in sub-Saharan Africa (LATA): a randomised, open-label, multicentre, 96-week non-inferiority trial
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01537-0/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

In adolescents with HIV with virological suppression, LAI cabotegravir–rilpivirine was superior to TLD for maintaining virological suppression, with no new safety concerns observed. These findings support LAI cabotegravir–rilpivirine as a maintenance treatment option for this population.

### [Articles] Radiotherapy versus observation following surgical resection of WHO grade 2 atypical meningioma (ROAM/EORTC-1308): an international, multicentre, open-label, phase 3, randomised controlled trial
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01804-0/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Our findings suggest that adjuvant radiotherapy should be considered in patients with atypical meningioma after complete surgical resection of atypical meningioma when the prevention of recurrence is valued higher than the potential delayed adverse effects of radiotherapy. This practice will benefit patients by reducing meningioma recurrence. Decisions should be based on individual discussion with the patient.

### [Articles] Retatrutide in adults with obesity and type 2 diabetes (TRIUMPH-2): a double-blind, parallel-group, randomised, placebo-controlled, phase 3 trial
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-14 03:03 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01861-1/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Treatment with retatrutide resulted in substantial improvements in bodyweight and was associated with improvements in glycaemic control in participants with obesity and type 2 diabetes, with a safety profile generally consistent with other molecules with GLP-1 receptor agonism. These results show that retatrutide might be effective for the treatment of obesity in people with type 2 diabetes.

### Hypernovae May Be All Hype, No Nova

Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Astrophysicists think that hypernovae are the most powerful stellar explosions in the Universe. They're the most explosive type of core-collapse supernova. But the evidence for them is not voluminous, and new research pokes holes in it, pointing out that hypernovae may not actually exist.

### On this day in space! Oct. 13, 1773: Astronomer Charles Messier discovers the Whirlpool Galaxy before we ever knew it was a galaxy
Source: Space.com
Published: 2026-10-13 21:00 UTC
URL: https://www.space.com/space-exploration/on-this-day-in-space-october-13-2026

In just one more month, the European Space Agency’s Hera mission will make history. Moving at 12 kilometers per second versus Earth, the mission is about to rendezvous with the binary Dimorphos-Didymos asteroid pair. NASA’s Double Asteroid Redirection Test (DART) mission slammed into Dimorphos in 2022, and now Hera will provide a follow up view of the mission’s resulting handiwork. The European Space Agency (ESA) recently released a thought-provoking look at the perspectives of Hera, and what an astronaut might see from the surface of the asteroids as the mission speeds by.

### Tiny Gold Nanogaps Could Crack the Chirality Puzzle
Source: Universe Today
Published: 2026-10-13 12:00 UTC
URL: https://www.universetoday.com/articles/tiny-gold-nanogaps-could-crack-the-chirality-puzzle
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Looking for biosignatures across the solar system requires looking for asymmetry. Amino acids, the building blocks of proteins, are famously either “left-handed” (L) or “right-handed” (D). We know abiotic processes (i.e. those that create amino acids but don’t involve life) create a 50/50 mixture of the two types, which is called a racemic mixture. But life as we know it almost exclusively relies on left-handed amino acids to fold proteins and right-handed ones to construct nucleic acids. So if we find an imbalance in the types of amino acids we analyze from somewhere in the solar system, it's a good indication that life is behind that imbalance. Differentiating between the two “chiralities”, as they’re called, is possible, but typically requires advanced equipment and chemical disposables that make them burdensome for spaceflight applications.

A new research paper published in Zootaxa describes five new species of freshwater mussels in the southern U.S., providing the scientific foundation for protecting the most imperiled group of animals in North America.

### DNA reveals chemosynthesis genes in bacteria living on coral reef fish gills
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-09-27 12:00 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-09-dna-reveals-chemosynthesis-genes-bacteria.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Chemosynthetic bacteria have been documented in various marine environments and in association with invertebrate hosts such as annelid worms and mollusks, but their presence in fish appears to be a new discovery. The findings show that fish gills host a complex and specialized microbiome that is not yet well understood but could play an important role in fish metabolism, immunity and health.

### Cuttlefish suckers reveal how they grip rough prey—and could inspire better technology
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-09-17 11:40 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-09-cuttlefish-suckers-reveal-rough-prey.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Cephalopods are a group of soft-bodied marine mollusks that include cuttlefish, octopuses, squid and nautiluses. Many of these mollusks have muscular suckers on their arms that allow them to seize prey or grip other surfaces in their surroundings.

### Pufferfish taste receptor more versatile than a human's, detecting both savory L- and sweet D-amino acids
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-09-16 15:20 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-09-pufferfish-receptor-versatile-human-savory.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Our sense of taste is critical for survival, alerting us to substances that provide vital nutrients and those that might poison us. Now, a team led by researchers from the University of Osaka has discovered how one such taste receptor in pufferfish can detect a surprisingly wide range of flavors at once. Their study is published in the Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences.

### Warming seas could shrink most mollusks by 2100, but some may grow
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-09-03 22:40 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-09-seas-mollusks.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Research led by scientists at the University of Louisiana at Lafayette challenges a long-held assumption about the effects of climate change on marine life: Warmer oceans won't simply make animals smaller.

### AI helps uncover oyster DNA changes linked to climate resilience
Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-08-27 13:00 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-08-ai-uncover-oyster-dna-linked.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

While a colleague traveled along the East Coast from Canada to the Gulf of Mexico collecting oysters, Northeastern University bioinformatics graduate student Megha Prasad worked away at her computer. She zeroed in on DNA, the molecule containing instructions for making proteins that shape the critters' traits. It also holds the key to their adaptation to the changing climate.
